2025-06-17T18:17:48.5584914Z *This section includes details on the pull request with code changes you have already submitted to fix the problem above.*
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5587133Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5587701Z <pr_title>
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5589211Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5591140Z </pr_title>
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5591947Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5592366Z <pr_description>
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5595382Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5596967Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5597137Z ## Problem
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5598132Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5598960Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5599512Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5600080Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5600651Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5601207Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5601675Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5601925Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5603374Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5604539Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5604705Z ## Solution
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5604930Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5605134Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5605790Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5606395Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5607104Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5608823Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5610017Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5610569Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5610745Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5611262Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5611607Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5611777Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5612169Z ```bash
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5613104Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5614175Z ```
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5614356Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5614528Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5614941Z ```bash
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5616017Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5617462Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5617834Z ```
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5617992Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5618226Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5618631Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5618784Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5619758Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5620641Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5620772Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5621183Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5621998Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5622856Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5623655Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5624561Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5625087Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5625663Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5626389Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5626504Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5626659Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5626663Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5626790Z </pr_description>
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5626973Z

2025-06-17T18:17:48.5727523Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5728041Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5728158Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5728381Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5728484Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5729002Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5729564Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5730184Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5730773Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5731142Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5731649Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5732193Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5732278Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5732568Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5732920Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5733244Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5733455Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5733554Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5734568Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5735514Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5735593Z ### 4. Verification
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5736283Z After setup, the workflow will automatically connect to your Tailscale network using the `tag:ci` tag, allowing secure access to your OT-2 simulator for testing Prefect installations.
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5736965Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5737483Z Let me know once you've completed the OAuth client setup and added the GitHub secrets, and I'll activate the Tailscale integration.

2025-06-17T18:17:48.5742249Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5742534Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5742908Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5743004Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5743152Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5743376Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5743708Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5743792Z ```json
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5743993Z {
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5744195Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5744431Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5744712Z   },
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5744899Z   "acls": [
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5745112Z     {
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5745312Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5745551Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5745788Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5746014Z     }
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5746199Z   ]
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5746382Z }
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5746565Z ```
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5746678Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5746766Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5746919Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5747314Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5747844Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5748371Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5748868Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5749011Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5749240Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5749409Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5749883Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5750439Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5750670Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5751270Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5751717Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5752030Z 
2025-06-17T18:17:48.5752564Z The Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub Actions runner when it connects to your tailnet.
